- Serine (Ser) - common nucleophile in proteases
- Cysteine (Cys) - common nucleophile in cysteine proteases
- Threonine (Thr) - nucleophile in threonine proteases
- Lysine (Lys) - nucleophile in some enzymes (forms Schiff bases with carbonyl compounds)

- D83 - Aspartate: typically acts as a catalytic base or stabilizes the oxyanion, not a nucleophile

<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which description best captures the supported state of the N-terminal cysteine pair in this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> The two cysteines are present as separate, reduced free thiols.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> The cysteines form a disulfide bond with a cysteine in another protein.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> The two cysteines form an intramolecular, redox-active disulfide bond.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Both cysteines are irreversibly oxidized and do not form a disulfide bond.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>First, I identified the cysteine residues in the protein sequence. There are exactly two cysteines located at positions 13 and 16, forming a **C-P-Y-C** (Cys-Pro-Tyr-Cys) motif at the N-terminus.<br><br>This C-P-Y-C motif is a specific variant of the **CXXC motif**, which is the hallmark of redox-active proteins such as glutaredoxins, thioredoxins, and protein disulfide isomerases. The CXXC motif functions as a catalytic center where the two cysteines undergo reversible oxidation and reduction to mediate thiol-disulfide exchange reactions.<br><br>Now evaluating each option:<br><br>- **Option A** (free thiols): While the reduced state exists transiently during the catalytic cycle, the &quot;supported&quot; (biologically relevant) state for this motif is the oxidized disulfide form, as the redox cycling between oxidized and reduced states is central to its function.<br><br>- **Option B** (intermolecular disulfide): The CXXC motif is specifically designed for *intramolecular* disulfide formation as part of its redox mechanism. There is no evidence supporting intermolecular pairing.<br><br>- **Option C** (intramolecular, redox-active disulfide): This is the textbook description of the CXXC motif. The two cysteines form a disulfide bond within the same protein that is reversibly reducible—this is precisely what makes it &quot;redox-active.&quot; The proximity and spacing (4 residues apart, forming a short loop) are ideal for intramolecular disulfide formation.<br><br>- **Option D** (irreversibly oxidized): Irreversible oxidation (e.g., to cysteine sulfonic acid) would destroy the protein&#x27;s function. This is not a biologically supported state for a redox-active motif.<br><br>The C-P-Y-C motif unambiguously represents a redox-active intramolecular disulfide bond.<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> The supplied sequence contains a closely spaced N-terminal cysteine pair. I&#x27;ll go with D as my answer. The C273-C284 disulfide bond is a well-supported structural feature of the CRD domain in this protein.
</pre>
